- **Last activity:** 2026-10-28, ITJamie, merged trunk into the branch. 9 days ago. No human comments.
- **Approvals:** none
- **CI:** green
- **Summary:** Adds a process-global cache, keyed by `registryID` and MSL version, for the [#10731](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10731) Intel workgroup probe result, so that repeated enumeration skips the 30 s probe. Fixes [#10742](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10742).
- **Findings:**
  1. [medium] It exists only to fix a cost that [#10731](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10731) introduces. It should be folded into [#10731](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10731), or replaced by a lazy per-device probe. A separate PR for a self-inflicted regression adds review overhead.
  2. [low] `wgpu-hal/src/metal/adapter.rs`: the first enumeration still pays the full probe cost. The global `static OnceCell` cache never clears. That is acceptable for its size, but it hides driver updates within a process.
  3. [low] `CHANGELOG.md`: the PR adds a new `#### Metal` heading at line ~317, probably in the wrong release section.
  4. [low] The branch contains the [#10731](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10731) commit `6cc39f8e`. It must be rebased after [#10731](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10731) lands.
- **Overlap and landing order:** [#10725](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10725) and [#10731](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10731) are independent on trunk, but both edit `wgpu-hal/src/metal/adapter.rs` (the import list, `CapabilitiesQuery`/`PrivateDisabilities::new`) and will have textual conflicts. [#10731](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10731) reads `private_caps.msl_version`, which [#10725](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10725) changes to the probed version, so on OCLP the two work together. Order: [#10725](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10725) (if accepted in an opt-in form) -> [#10731](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10731) -> [#10743](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10743), or squash [#10743](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10743) into [#10731](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10731). [#10743](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10743) cannot land before [#10731](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10731).
- **To land:** [#10731](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10731) lands first. Then squash this PR into it, or rebase it.
- **Move-along action:** ask the author to fold [#10743](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10743) into [#10731](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10731) (same decision gate as [#10731](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10731)).

#### [#10677](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10677): vulkan: Give every semaphore a value when chaining timeline submit info ([@laurenz-canva](https://github.com/laurenz-canva))

- **Summary:** Adds `Workarounds::PAD_TIMELINE_SEMAPHORE_VALUES` for `IMAGINATION_PROPRIETARY`. When it is set and a submit has timeline values, every wait and signal semaphore gets a value. This works around the PowerVR driver, which reads `pWaitSemaphoreValues[i]` without checking the count. It fixes a crash on Pixel 10 and Teclast BXM, and probably [#7669](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/7669).

A maintainer must decide: keep it gated to the IMG driver, add gfxstream, or turn it on for all drivers as a documented workaround. The padding is spec-legal and costs very little.
  3. [low] There are no tests. leekichko offered 4 unit tests for `SemaphoreList::add_to_submit` (commit bce0159 on leekichko/wgpu). The author can cherry-pick them.

#### [#10750](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10750): Add opt-in static HDR metadata submission for Vulkan surfaces ([@ecke101](https://github.com/ecke101))
- **Verdict:** Needs a lot of work
- **Waiting on:** author (CI is red), and maintainer for the policy/design decision (jimblandy is assigned)
- **Last activity:** 2026-10-27, ecke101, opened the PR. There are no comments or reviews. 10 days.
- **Approvals:** none
- **CI:** red. `Test Linux x86_64` fails, and the failure is related. Four `wgpu-benchmark` tests abort on `VUID-vkCreateDevice-ppEnabledExtensionNames-01387`: "Missing extension required to enable device extension VK_EXT_hdr_metadata: VK_KHR_swapchain".
- **Summary:** Adds `wgt::HdrMetadata`, `Features::SURFACE_HDR_METADATA` (bit 16), `Surface::supports_hdr_metadata` and `Surface::set_hdr_metadata`. It also adds new `wgpu_hal::Surface` trait methods. The metadata goes through `vkSetHdrMetadataEXT` on native Vulkan swapchains.
- **Findings:**
  1. [high] `wgpu-hal/src/vulkan/adapter.rs:1117` The feature is exposed whenever `VK_EXT_hdr_metadata` is supported, and `get_required_extensions` pushes it. `required_device_extensions` drops `VK_KHR_swapchain` when the adapter lacks it, so a device can enable `hdr_metadata` without its required `VK_KHR_swapchain`. This causes the CI failure. The feature must also require swapchain support.
  2. [high] Policy: the description says "An agent implemented and reviewed the code, executed the checks, and created this PR". The contributor wrote only one paragraph. Per AGENTS.md and CONTRIBUTING, fully agentic PRs are not allowed.
  3. [medium] API design: both `set_hdr_metadata` and `supports_hdr_metadata` return a bare `bool`, which can mean any of 6 failure causes. There is no error type and nothing goes to the device error sink. The PR also adds two default-impl methods to the public `wgpu_hal::Surface` trait and a `SurfaceInterface` dispatch method, all for a Vulkan-only feature. It does not consider DX12 (`IDXGISwapChain4::SetHDRMetaData`) or Metal (`CAEDRMetadata`) for the shape of the API.
  4. [medium] Metadata is not kept across `configure`. The app must re-apply it after every reconfigure, and the type system does not enforce this. A failed `set_hdr_metadata` is silent.
  5. [low] There are no GPU tests, only unit tests of `is_valid` and the opt-in extension list.
- **To land:** fix the extension dependency, get a maintainer decision on agentic-PR policy and on whether wgpu wants this API shape, and then a real design review.
- **Move-along action:** decide whether to accept a fully agentic PR and this API shape. If not, close it (agentic-PR policy) and point the author to a design issue.
- **Depth:** full for the hal/core/wgpu parts; skimmed the `HdrMetadata` unit tests.

#### [#10837](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10837): vulkan: write the pass-begin timestamp at TOP_OF_PIPE ([@kikijiki](https://github.com/kikijiki))
- **Verdict:** Land as is
- **Waiting on:** maintainer (no reviewer or assignee yet)
- **Last activity:** 2026-11-04, kikijiki, opened the PR. 2 days.
- **Approvals:** none
- **CI:** green
- **Summary:** Writes `beginning_of_pass_write_index` for render and compute passes with `TOP_OF_PIPE` instead of `BOTTOM_OF_PIPE`, through a new private helper. The end-of-pass stamp and the in-pass `write_timestamp` stay unchanged. The author measured near-zero "void" pass durations on RADV under GPU contention, and none after the change.
- **Findings:**
  1. [low] `wgpu-hal/src/vulkan/command.rs:42` With `TOP_OF_PIPE`, the begin stamp can latch before earlier passes finish. Pass N can then appear to start before pass N-1 ends, so durations include queue wait and overlap. Most GPU profilers make the same trade-off, and it is better than the zero durations of today. A maintainer should accept the semantic change knowingly.
  2. [low] The fix is empirical, from one GPU (RADV), and has no test. The bug is timing-dependent, so a deterministic test is hard to write. This is acceptable.
- **To land:** nothing.
- **Move-along action:** review (small, two-file change; likely merge).
- **Depth:** full

#### [#10840](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10840): Vulkan: Don't use timeline semaphores on Arm's proprietary Mali driver ([@tadeohepperle](https://github.com/tadeohepperle))
- **Verdict:** Needs a bit of work
- **Waiting on:** maintainer (no reviewer or assignee yet)
- **Last activity:** 2026-11-05, tadeohepperle, opened the PR. 1 day.
- **Approvals:** none
- **CI:** green
- **Summary:** Adds `Workarounds::DISABLE_TIMELINE_SEMAPHORES` for `ARM_PROPRIETARY`, which makes wgpu fall back to the binary `FencePool`. Without it, the Mali driver uses 16 B of kernel memory per timeline signal and loses the device after about 4.2M submissions. The PR also adds a 297-line `examples/bug-repro/03_arm_timeline_semaphore_leak` crate.
- **Findings:**
  1. [medium] `CHANGELOG.md` has a placeholder link: `[#XXXXX](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/pull/XXXXX)`.
  2. [medium] `wgpu-hal/src/vulkan/adapter.rs:2372` The flag is on for every Arm proprietary driver version, but only `g18p0` was tested. The Arm proprietary driver runs most Mali Android devices, so the change affects a large population. The author's own data shows an alternative: destroy and recreate the timeline semaphore periodically. That keeps timeline semantics but adds complexity. A maintainer must choose.
  3. [low] The repro crate is a workspace and default member, so CI builds it and it adds to `Cargo.lock`. This follows the existing `bug-repro/01`, `02` pattern. It is the maintainer's choice whether to keep it.
  4. [low] Bit `1 << 3` collides with [#10692](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10692).
- **To land:** fix the CHANGELOG PR number, decide between disabling timeline semaphores on all Arm proprietary versions and the recreate approach, and keep or drop the repro crate.
- **Move-along action:** review, and decide whether disabling timeline semaphores on every Arm proprietary driver is acceptable.
- **Depth:** full for the hal change; skimmed the repro crate.

#### [#10629](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10629): Vulkan multi-planar DMA-buf import support ([@andyleiserson](https://github.com/andyleiserson))
- **Verdict:** Needs a bit of work
- **Waiting on:** author (description says "Testing TBD"; CHANGELOG has `#TODO`), then reviewer inner-daemons (review requested)
- **Last activity:** 2026-11-04, andyleiserson, pushed a "fmt" commit. There are no comments or reviews. 2 days.
- **Approvals:** none
- **CI:** green
- **Summary:** Adds multi-plane DMA-buf import to the Vulkan hal. `texture_from_dmabuf_fd` now takes a slice of plane layouts and an `alias` flag. The new `texture_from_dmabuf_fds` imports disjoint images. The PR adds `ImportTextureError` and replaces `TextureMemory::Dedicated` with `DeviceMemory(Vec<_>)`. It is for Firefox external textures and fixes [#9801](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9801).
- **Findings:**
  1. [medium] There are no tests. The multi-fd disjoint path is untested even locally, because the author's setup does not support it. The author has LLM-written tests on a local branch.
  2. [medium] `wgpu-hal/src/vulkan/device.rs` (`import_disjoint_dmabuf_memory`) When several planes share one fd, `allocation_size` is the maximum of the per-plane `req.size`. The memory is bound at offset 0, and the plane offsets are relative to the start of the DMA-buf. If a driver's per-plane size does not include the plane offset, the allocation is too small. The DMA-buf size (`lseek(fd, 0, SEEK_END)`) is a safer bound.
  3. [low] `wgpu-hal/src/vulkan/device.rs` (`texture_from_dmabuf_fds`) When `external_memory_dma_buf` or `image_drm_format_modifier` is missing, the error reports `MissingExtension(khr::external_memory_fd::NAME)`. This is the wrong extension name.
  4. [low] `CHANGELOG.md` has `[#TODO]` and a stray ". ." The PR checklist is all unchecked, and the fixup commits ("Windows build fix", "View format fix", "fmt") must be squashed before the rebase-merge (label `merge: rebase`).
  5. [low] The Vulkan 1.1 check covers only the disjoint path. The author raises this as an open question in the description, and a maintainer should answer it.
- **To land:** add tests (at least single-fd multi-plane, and the error paths), fix the CHANGELOG link, squash the fixups, fix the extension name in the error, and check the shared-fd allocation size.
- **Move-along action:** ping author (is it ready for review? add the tests).
- **Depth:** targeted (read the import/bind/cleanup paths and the error handling; skimmed the `create_vk_image` refactor and the `ImportTextureError` definitions)

**Deeper review ([#10629](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10629): Vulkan multi-planar DMA-buf import support):**

> - **Open questions from the first pass:**
>   1. When several planes share one fd in the disjoint path, can `allocation_size` be too small?
>   2. Does one error report the wrong extension name?
> - **Answers:**
>   1. [refuted for conforming drivers; a hardening option remains] `wgpu-hal/src/vulkan/device.rs:850-871` sets `allocation_size` to the max of the per-plane `req.size`, and `:892-904` binds every plane at `memory_offset(0)`. The Vulkan spec (`chapters/resources.adoc`, `vkGetImageSubresourceLayout` with DRM modifier tiling) says: "If the image is _disjoint_, then the pname:offset is relative to the base address of the _memory plane_." So for a disjoint image, the explicit plane-layout offset is inside that plane's own binding, and binding at 0 with the DMA-buf offset is correct. The per-plane `VkMemoryRequirements.size` describes that whole binding, so a conforming driver must include the offset in it. Otherwise an allocation of the reported size would not cover the plane. The first-pass concern applies only to a driver bug. Using `lseek(fd, 0, SEEK_END)` as an extra bound would be optional hardening. Reasoned from the spec. I did not check Mesa sources and did not run anything. The code comment at `:869-870` ("offsets are relative to the start of the DMA-buf") is correct only because the binding is at 0. A link to the spec sentence would help reviewers.
>   2. [confirmed] `wgpu-hal/src/vulkan/device.rs:719-726` checks `ext::external_memory_dma_buf` and `ext::image_drm_format_modifier`, but returns `MissingExtension(ash::khr::external_memory_fd::NAME)`. It should report whichever of the two is missing. The second check at `:728-735` (no `external_memory_fd` function table) correctly reports `khr::external_memory_fd`.
> - **New findings:** none.
> - **Verdict change:** none (Needs a bit of work). First-pass finding 2 drops from medium to low (optional hardening).
> - **Move-along action change:** none. In "To land", replace "check the shared-fd allocation size" with "optional: bound the allocation by the DMA-buf size".
>
> ---
>
> Sources used for the DX12 questions:
> - [IDXGISwapChain2::GetFrameLatencyWaitableObject](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/dxgi1_3/nf-dxgi1_3-idxgiswapchain2-getframelatencywaitableobject)
> - [IDXGISwapChain2::SetMaximumFrameLatency](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/dxgi1_3/nf-dxgi1_3-idxgiswapchain2-setmaximumframelatency)
> - [IDXGISwapChain::ResizeBuffers](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/dxgi/nf-dxgi-idxgiswapchain-resizebuffers)
> - [DXGI_PRESENT](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3ddxgi/dxgi-present)
> - [Reduce latency with DXGI 1.3 swap chains](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/uwp/gaming/reduce-latency-with-dxgi-1-3-swap-chains)
> - vkd3d-proton `libs/vkd3d/swapchain.c` (master), DXVK `src/dxgi/dxgi_swapchain.cpp` (master): secondary evidence, emulation only.

## GLES, DX12 and platform


Date of pass: 2026-11-06. Read only. No builds or tests run. Local diffs read from `refs/review/pr-N`.

#### [#10805](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10805): Fix FXC shader cache use-after-free across threads, fixes [#10547](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10547) ([@xorza](https://github.com/xorza))
- **Verdict:** Land as is
- **Waiting on:** maintainer (any reviewer). inner-daemons is the requested reviewer but has only commented on the issue link.
- **Last activity:** 2026-11-02, xorza, merged trunk into the branch (CI rerun). inner-daemons noted a wrong issue link on 2026-11-01; the link is now correct ([#10547](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10547)). 4 days.
- **Approvals:** none
- **CI:** green
- **Summary:** `dx12::CompiledShader` changes from an enum of COM blobs (DXC, FXC, precompiled Vec) to an `Arc<[u8]>` copy of the bytecode. The shader cache then never clones FXC's `ID3DBlob`, whose AddRef and Release are not atomic (disassembly in [#10547](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10547)).
- **Findings:**
  1. [low] `wgpu-hal/src/dx12/shader_compilation.rs:450` One extra bytecode copy per compile. This cost is negligible next to compilation, and the change also makes DXC safe without proof that DXC's count is atomic. No action.
  2. [low] No regression test. The race shows only under page heap and many threads (see [#10547](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10547)), so a test is not practical. Acceptable.
- **To land:** nothing.
- **Move-along action:** merge (or approve and queue). The PR is small and the root cause in [#10547](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10547) is well supported. It is a real UAF for multi-threaded apps on FXC/WARP.
- **Depth:** full

#### [#10775](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10775): feat(hal/dx12): Extension to restrict enumeration to a specific adapter LUID ([@andyleiserson](https://github.com/andyleiserson))
- **Verdict:** Needs a bit of work (a design decision is open; the code is in good shape)
- **Waiting on:** maintainer. Andy asked for input on 2026-11-03 about the unsafe-to-construct `Extensions<T>` token plus a separate `request_adapters` argument. No reviewer is assigned and no review exists.
- **Last activity:** 2026-11-04, andyleiserson, pushed "fix wasm" after their 2026-11-03 request for design input. 2 days.
- **Approvals:** none
- **CI:** green
- **Summary:** Lands part of the [#10304](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10304) extension API. It adds `wgt::BackendMap`, `BackendAdapterOptionsMap`, an `Extensions<T>` token that is unsafe to construct, `hal::Api::AdapterOptions`, an `options` argument on `hal::Instance::enumerate_adapters`, `dx12::Dx12AdapterOptions { luid }`, and a new public `wgpu::Instance::request_adapters(options, extensions)`. It has a DX12 GPU test for select-by-LUID and for unknown-LUID giving `NotFound`. Fixes [#9932](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9932).
- **Findings:**
  1. [medium] Design question for the maintainer: one token type `Extensions<T>` gates all extensions. Some are safe (`Dx12AdapterOptions` docs say "These options are safe"), and all still need `unsafe`. Andy says the `request_adapter` / `request_adapters` split bothers them more than the token. This needs a decision before merge, because it sets the pattern for every future extension ([#10304](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10304), [#4067](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/4067)).
  2. [low] `CHANGELOG.md` covers only the LUID filter. It does not mention the new public `wgpu::Instance::request_adapters`, `wgpu::Extensions`, `BackendAdapterOptionsMap`, or the breaking `hal::Instance::enumerate_adapters` signature (that matters for out-of-tree hal backends).
  3. [low] `wgpu-hal/src/lib.rs` The doc on `Api::AdapterOptions` says "used with `request_adapter_ext`", but that function does not exist.
  4. [low] `wgpu-core/src/instance.rs` `request_adapters` now has its own `profiling::scope!` and `api_log!` with the name "Instance::request_adapter". `request_adapter` calls it, so each call logs twice under the same name.
- **To land:** a design decision on the token and API shape. CHANGELOG entries for the new public API. A fix for the stale doc reference.
- **Move-along action:** decide whether the `Extensions<T>` token plus separate-argument shape is the extension pattern, then review (or assign a reviewer).
- **Depth:** targeted (read the core, hal, wgpu API surface, DX12 filter, test and the first half of `wgpu-types/src/backend.rs`; skipped the `backend_map_type!` / `adapter_options!` macro bodies and the per-backend boilerplate)

**Deeper review ([#10775](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10775): feat(hal/dx12): Extension to restrict enumeration to a specific adapter LUID):**

> - **Open questions from the first pass:**
>   1. Review the skipped `backend_map_type!` / `backend_type!` macro bodies and the per-backend boilerplate.
>   2. Can safe code construct a non-empty `Extensions<T>`, or smuggle an extension to the wrong backend?
> - **Answers:**
>   1. [reviewed] `wgpu-types/src/backend.rs:390-447` `backend_map_type!`: a `const` assert ties it to the crate root. It generates a `CheckTrait`/`CheckStruct<const B: usize>` pair, so two value types for one backend in the same crate fail with a conflicting-impl error. The inner macro implements `Sealed<M>`, `BackendMapValue<M>` (with `BACKEND`) and `DynBackendMapValue<M>`. The matcher takes the struct body as one `tt`, so braced and unit structs work. Tuple and generic structs do not, which is acceptable. `Backend` discriminants are 0..=5 and `Backend::COUNT` is 6 (`backend.rs:80`), so `T::BACKEND as usize` stays in bounds. The per-backend boilerplate (Vulkan, Metal, GL, Noop types, an `_options` parameter on each `enumerate_adapters`, two example call sites passing `None`) is mechanical and correct. The DX12 filter compares `GetDesc().AdapterLuid` and drops an adapter if `GetDesc` fails, with an error log. That is reasonable.
>   2. [refuted: no safe construction and no wrong-backend delivery; a panic is possible] `wgpu-types/src/tokens.rs:13`: `Extensions<T>(Option<T>)` has a private field. The only constructors are `Default` (empty) and `unsafe fn new` (`:27`). It derives `Clone`/`Copy`/`Eq`/`PartialEq`/`Debug`, which cannot make a new non-empty value. It implements `Deref` to `Option<T>` (`:32`) and not `DerefMut`, so safe code cannot change the contents. `BackendMap` has no interior mutability. So safe code cannot construct a non-empty token or change one after construction.
>
>      Wrong backend: `BackendMap::set` indexes by `T::BACKEND`, and the hal reads with `map.get::<A::AdapterOptions>()` (`wgpu-hal/src/dynamic/instance.rs:64`). `get` downcasts through `Any` and checks the `TypeId`, so a backend can never receive another type as its own options. The seal is only partial: `__backend_map_private` is `pub` (`backend.rs:235`, "quasi-seal"). A downstream crate can implement `Sealed`/`BackendMapValue` for its own type with `BACKEND = Dx12` and put it in the map. Then `get` reaches `downcast_ref::<T>().unwrap()` (`backend.rs:298`, also `remove` at `:306`) and panics during `request_adapters`. This needs deliberate misuse, and an `unsafe { Extensions::new(..) }` call to get there, and the result is a panic, not UB. Using `.and_then(|v| v.downcast_ref())` (ignore foreign values) or documenting the panic would close it.
> - **New findings:**
>   1. [low-medium] `wgpu/src/backend/wgpu_core.rs:854` changes `request_adapter` from `apply_limit_buckets: false` to `options.apply_limit_buckets`. On trunk, `wgpu::Instance::request_adapter` ignores the user's `apply_limit_buckets` (`wgpu-types/src/adapter.rs:58`). This PR quietly fixes that, so adapters change for users who set it. It is user-visible, it is not in the CHANGELOG, and it is not related to the LUID filter. It should be its own commit with a CHANGELOG entry.
>   2. [low] `wgpu-types/src/backend.rs:480` `backend_type!` is `#[macro_export]`ed (public API) but nothing uses it. Its doc example names `wgt::BackendDeviceOptions`, which does not exist. Drop it until a user exists.
>   3. [low] `backend.rs:298,306` the `unwrap` panic described in answer 2.
>   4. [nit] `BackendAdapterOptions` (`wgpu-types/src/adapter.rs`) is only a type-level tag for `BackendMap<dyn BackendAdapterOptions>`. No value type implements it. The `BackendMap` doc says that `SomeTrait` "is implemented by the value type for each backend", which is not true here. Fix the doc or implement the trait in the inner macro.
> - **Verdict change:** none (Needs a bit of work; the design decision on the token is still open). The token itself is sound as an "unsafe to construct" gate.
> - **Move-along action change:** none. Add new finding 1 to the review notes.

#### [#10554](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10554): gles/wgl: request an explicit core version from wglCreateContextAttribsARB ([@jimm98y](https://github.com/jimm98y))
- **Verdict:** Needs a bit of work
- **Waiting on:** maintainer (Wumpf). On 2026-10-25 the author approved Wumpf's suggestion and asked "is there anything else I should do?". Nobody replied. The concrete fixes are mechanical, but the author is not a Rust developer and pushes from their fork's `trunk`, so maintainers cannot push.
- **Last activity:** 2026-10-25, jimm98y, asked what else to do after Wumpf's CHANGES_REQUESTED review. 12 days.
- **Approvals:** none (Wumpf: CHANGES_REQUESTED 2026-10-25)
- **CI:** red. "Format & Typos" fails. This is related: commits `d3493063` and `4a12198e` carry `Co-Authored-By: Claude` trailers, which Wumpf linked as the failure. mergeable_state is dirty (conflicts, probably CHANGELOG).
- **Summary:** The WGL path asks for core contexts 4.6 down to 3.3, and falls back to the version-less request only at the end. It also retries the first `wglCreateContext` once. This fixes the Parallels-on-M4 driver, which returns GL 2.1 and so made wgpu fall back to WARP.
- **Findings:**
  1. [medium] Process: the PR was agent-created and agent-described. The "self-reviewed" box is unchecked, and the author says they cannot judge Rust. ErichDonGubler flagged the LLM policy. Wumpf then checked the approach independently and ended at the same fix, so a maintainer now vouches for the code.
  2. [low] `wgpu-hal/src/gles/wgl.rs` The retry of `wglCreateContext` is a blind second attempt that only Parallels needs. It is cheap and harmless, but there is no evidence beyond one VM.
  3. [low] The explicit 4.6 to 3.3 loop changes context creation on every Windows GL driver. The risk is low, because the drivers that matter return the highest version for any explicit request. There is no hardware test beyond the reporter's VM.
- **To land:** remove the AI co-author trailers (or squash with a clean message), rebase onto trunk, make CI green.
- **Move-along action:** take over. Wumpf already said they would clean it up but cannot push. Re-create the PR from a maintainer branch with one clean commit and credit jimm98y. The other option is to reply to the author with the exact steps (squash, drop the trailers, rebase), but they may not manage them.
- **Depth:** full

#### [#10778](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10778): Make `wayland-sys`'s `dlopen` feature togglable ([@HigherOrderLogic](https://github.com/HigherOrderLogic))
- **Verdict:** Needs a lot of work
- **Waiting on:** author (CI red). No reviewer is assigned and nobody has commented. A maintainer decision on whether to accept the feature is also needed.
- **Last activity:** 2026-10-29, HigherOrderLogic, opened the PR. 8 days.
- **Approvals:** none
- **CI:** red: Clippy Linux x86_64/aarch64, Clippy MacOS x86_64/aarch64, Clippy IOS, Clippy Android aarch64, Clippy OpenHarmony, Clippy cts_runner, Core MSRV Linux/MacOS, Test Linux x86_64, Test Mac aarch64, API Validation/Operation Linux, Other Linux. Related. When the feature is off, `wayland-sys` (a `cfg(unix)` dep) links to libwayland at compile time on every unix target.
- **Summary:** Moves `wayland-sys/dlopen` behind a new `wgpu-hal/wayland-dlopen` feature, which is on in `wgpu`'s default features only, and adds direct-link imports in `gles/egl.rs`.
- **Findings:**
  1. [high] `wgpu-hal/Cargo.toml` `wgpu-hal` without the new feature (wgpu-core and wgpu-hal users such as Firefox and Servo, and `wgpu` users with `default-features = false`, for example Bevy) now links libwayland-client and libwayland-egl at build time. That breaks builds on macOS, iOS and Android, and adds a hard runtime dep on X11-only Linux. Dynamic loading must stay the default for every crate, not only for `wgpu`'s defaults.
  2. [medium] Cargo features are additive, so "link at compile time" can only win when nobody in the graph enables `wayland-dlopen`. The design needs a maintainer decision. Options: an opt-in `wayland-link` feature with dlopen as the fallback in code, or no change (use `wayland-sys`'s own features from the app).
  3. [low] Missing CHANGELOG entry for a new `wgpu` feature.
  4. [low] `wgpu-hal/src/gles/egl.rs` The `#[cfg(all(unix, not(feature = "wayland-dlopen")))]` imports are not limited to Linux/BSD, which adds to the non-Linux breakage.
- **To land:** a maintainer decision that the feature is wanted. Keep dlopen the default for wgpu-hal and wgpu-core. Fix CI on all targets. Add a CHANGELOG entry.
- **Move-along action:** decide whether wgpu should offer compile-time libwayland linking at all. If yes, ping the author with the "dlopen stays default at the hal level" constraint. If no, close it with that reason.
- **Depth:** full

All 19 of cwfitzgerald's self-review threads from 2026-06-30 are resolved. inner-daemons is still a requested reviewer but has only posted "will get to this" comments (2026-08-05, 2026-08-21).

- **Open threads (andyleiserson, 27):** these are mostly minor (docs, naming, helper shape, CHANGELOG wording, two suggested CHANGELOG edits). The substantive ones:
  - DLL and COM drop order in `DxgiInstance`, `DeviceShared` and `DxgiSurface` (`swapchain/dxgi.rs:293`).
  - An untyped `transmute` relying on a niche (`dxgi.rs:214`).
  - Swapchain reuse across devices and alpha-mode changes on resize (`dxgi.rs:601`).
  - A leak of back buffers on a partial construction failure (`dxgi.rs:757`).
  - A missing per-format and semaphore external-handle capability query (`instance.rs:1274`).
  - The undocumented downgrade when DXGI is unavailable (`vulkan/mod.rs:193`).
  - A referenced HLSL test that does not exist (`dxgi_sync.hlsl:10`).
  - In the review body: a possible submit-failure deadlock on the interop fence, and a possible queue family transfer.
- **Move-along action:** author work: rebase, address Andy's threads, re-request review from andyleiserson. Consider dropping inner-daemons as a requested reviewer.

## Core: textures and resources


Reference date: 2026-11-06. CI data comes from the check runs of each PR head.

Dependencies:
- [#10776](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10776) is stacked on [#10710](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10710). The [#10776](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10776) head contains the [#10710](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10710) commit `62998461` unchanged. [#10776](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10776) can land only after [#10710](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10710). The author asks for rebase after [#10710](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10710) merges.
- [#10795](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10795) and [#10812](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10812) do not depend on each other. Both are based on `97474455`, and both are building blocks for [#5138](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/5138) (Firefox/Servo presentation). They are compatible: [#10812](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10812) changes `initialization_status` to `Arc<RwLock<..>>`, and `mark_uninitialized` from [#10795](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10795) still compiles against it through `Deref`. On a child texture, `mark_uninitialized` resets the tracker that the child shares with its parent. That looks intended. Expect a small textual conflict in `wgpu-core/src/resource.rs`.

#### [#8014](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/8014): Implement webgpu textureBindingViewDimension proposal to resolve texture view heuristics errors ([@alextechcc](https://github.com/alextechcc))
- **Verdict:** Needs a bit of work
- **Waiting on:** other reviewer (teoxoy, assignee). teoxoy requested changes on 2026-10-30 against `6c793998`. The author force-pushed `a7817bee` on 2026-10-31. The push adds the `CUBE_ARRAY_TEXTURES` downlevel check and the check that the layer count is a multiple of 6, which is what teoxoy asked for. The author posted no comment and did not re-request review.
- **Last activity:** 2026-10-31, alextechcc, force-push that addresses teoxoy's review. 6 days.
- **Approvals:** none. teoxoy: CHANGES_REQUESTED (on the old commit).
- **CI:** green on Linux and Windows. All macOS jobs were cancelled (Test Mac, API/Other Mac, Clippy macOS/iOS/visionOS, MSRV macOS). This looks like a runner problem and not related to the PR, but macOS has no result for this head.
- **Summary:** Adds `TextureDescriptor::texture_binding_view_dimension` and `DownlevelFlags::ARBITRARY_BINDING_VIEW_DIMENSIONS`. On GLES, the GL target comes from this field and not from the old cube heuristic, and wgpu-core rejects bind group views of any other dimension.
- **Findings:**
  1. [high] `wgpu-core/src/device/resource.rs:~2112` The texture creation check only covers `D2`, `Cube` and `CubeArray`, and the `_ => {}` arm accepts any other value without checking the texture `dimension`. Some descriptors that pass:
     - a 2D texture with `Some(D3)`, which gets a `TEXTURE_3D` target;
     - a 3D texture with `Some(D2Array)`;
     - a 3D texture with depth 6 and `Some(Cube)`, which gets `TEXTURE_CUBE_MAP`;
     - a non-square texture with `Cube`/`CubeArray`.

     The GLES HAL takes the value as given (`gles/mod.rs` `get_info_from_desc`). wgpu-core must check that the value is compatible with `dimension`, and that the size is square for cube values.
  2. [medium] `CHANGELOG.md` The default behavior on GLES/WebGL2 changes silently. A 6-layer cube-compatible texture (or one whose layer count is a multiple of 6) used to become a cube map. Now it becomes `D2Array`, and binding it as `Cube` fails validation unless the user sets the new field. Skybox code on WebGL (for example Bevy) breaks. The changelog explains the field but does not say that cube textures on GLES now need it.
  3. [medium] `wgpu-core/src/device/resource.rs:~2109` On adapters with `ARBITRARY_BINDING_VIEW_DIMENSIONS`, wgpu-core does not check the field at all. A descriptor that GLES rejects is then accepted on Vulkan, Metal and DX12, so a portability bug shows up only on GL. Consider checking the layer count and dimension compatibility on all backends, and enforcing the binding match only when the flag is not present.
  4. [low] `wgpu-hal/src/gles/device.rs:1512` The old `log_failing_target_heuristics` is now a `debug_assert_eq!`. That is correct only if wgpu-core covers every binding path. Storage and sampled bindings are checked. Confirm that external textures and `create_texture_from_hal` (which passes `None`) cannot reach this assert on GLES.
  5. [low] The PR touches 110 files. Most of them only add `texture_binding_view_dimension: None` to tests and examples. This is expected for a new struct field, but the author's August note says that some unrelated tests were skipped. That needs a check before merge. This pass did not find an added `skip`, but it did not read every test file.
- **To land:** add dimension-compatibility and square-size checks with validation tests; add a CHANGELOG line about the GLES cube default change; get teoxoy's re-review.
- **Move-along action:** ping teoxoy to re-review the 2026-10-31 push, and pass on findings 1 to 3.
- **Depth:** targeted (core validation, GLES HAL, types and changelog read; the about 90 test and example files that only add the field were skipped)

**Deeper review ([#8014](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/8014): Implement webgpu textureBindingViewDimension proposal to resolve texture view heuristics errors):**

> - **Open questions from the first pass:**
>   1. Do the skipped test files hide anything (for example an added `skip`)?
>   2. Does wgpu-core validate the new field against the texture dimension and size (for example, a 3D texture with `Cube`)?
>   3. What does the WebGPU proposal text require?
>   4. Does the 2026-10-31 force-push address all of teoxoy's 2026-10-30 review points?
> - **Answers:**
>   1. **[refuted: no hidden skips]** I checked every test and example file in the diff (`a7817bee~3..a7817bee`, 84 files under tests/examples). In all but 12 files, the only change is `texture_binding_view_dimension: None,`. No `skip`, `expect_fail` or `FailureCase` is added. The files with other changes:
>      - `tests/tests/wgpu-gpu/texture_binding_view_dimension.rs`: three tests. Layer-count rejections (D2 with 2 layers, Cube with 5, CubeArray with 7; CubeArray with 6 or 12 layers when `CUBE_ARRAY_TEXTURES` is missing) and sampled/storage bind-group mismatches. All parents are `TextureDimension::D2` with a 4x4 size.
>      - `tests/tests/wgpu-gpu/regression/issue_7428.rs`: a 1-layer D2 texture marked `D2Array`, bound as `texture_2d_array`, and read back.
>      - `examples/features/src/skybox/mod.rs`: sets `Some(Cube)` for the skybox.
>      - `examples/features/src/multiview/mod.rs:77`: only a comment, "Not usable on GLES/WebGL: the blitter binds single-layer `D2` views of this array texture". The old heuristic was also wrong for this case, so this is not a new break.
>      - `deno_webgpu`: maps the field through.
>      - The rest are API plumbing in `wgpu`.
>   2. **[confirmed]** `wgpu-core/src/device/resource.rs:2108-2151` checks only:
>      - `D2` → 1 layer;
>      - `Cube` → 6 layers;
>      - `CubeArray` → `CUBE_ARRAY_TEXTURES` and a multiple of 6 layers;
>      - `_ => {}` (`resource.rs:2148`).
>
>      Nothing compares the value with `desc.dimension`, and nothing checks for a square size. Descriptors that pass on GLES:
>      - a 3D texture with depth 6 and `Cube`;
>      - a 3D texture with depth 1 and `D2`;
>      - a 2D texture with `D3`;
>      - a 3D texture with `D2Array`;
>      - a non-square 2D texture with 6 layers and `Cube`.
>
>      The GLES HAL uses the value as the allocation target (`wgpu-hal/src/gles/mod.rs:515` `get_info_from_desc` → `target_for_view_dimension`). For example, a non-square `TEXTURE_CUBE_MAP` makes `glTexStorage2D` fail with `GL_INVALID_VALUE`, so the texture gets no storage, and later uploads and copies hit GL errors. I did not find a memory-safety problem (WebGL is safe, native GL gives GL errors), but invalid input reaches the HAL, which wgpu-hal does not allow. The tests use only `D2` parents with square sizes, so they miss this.
>   3. **[answered]** The current spec text (`webgpu-spec.bs`, compat blocks):
>      - "validating GPUTextureDescriptor": on devices without `core-features-and-limits`, `"2d"` needs `depthOrArrayLayers == 1`, `"cube"` needs `depthOrArrayLayers == 6`, and `"cube-array"` is not allowed at all.
>      - `createTexture`: the default is `1d`/`2d`/`2d-array`/`3d` from the dimension and the layer count.
>      - `createBindGroup`: sampled and storage views must have `dimension == textureBindingViewDimension`.
>
>      The spec does **not** check compatibility with `dimension` or a square size. In WebGPU this is harmless, because a cube view of a 3D texture can never be created, so that texture can never be bound. So the PR matches the spec, apart from its intentional `CubeArray` extension (which teoxoy asked for). The missing checks are needed because wgpu's GLES HAL allocates storage from this value, not because the spec requires them. That is a wgpu-specific requirement, and it should be stated in the review.
>   4. **[confirmed]** teoxoy's 2026-10-30 review (on `6c793998`) has only a body and no inline comments. It asks for two things: allow `CubeArray` when `DownlevelFlags::CUBE_ARRAY_TEXTURES` is present, and require the layer count to be a multiple of 6. Both are in `a7817bee` (`resource.rs:2139-2147`) and are tested (`texture_binding_view_dimension.rs:44-59`). All of teoxoy's points are addressed. The author did not reply or re-request review.
> - **New findings:**
>   1. [low] `create_texture_from_hal` resolves a default dimension for the HAL texture (`resource.rs:1670`). A user who wraps an existing GL cube map and passes `None` can no longer bind it as `Cube`, because core rejects it. The user must set the field. This should be mentioned with the CHANGELOG finding.
>   2. [low] The GLES `debug_assert_eq!` (`wgpu-hal/src/gles/device.rs:1514`) is reachable only if core lets a mismatched view through. Both bind paths check the resolved dimension, and core rejects view dimensions that are incompatible with the texture dimension. So I found no path to this assert. The first pass's finding 4 is refuted.
> - **Verdict change:** none (Needs a bit of work).
> - **Move-along action change:** none. Ping teoxoy to re-review. When passing on finding 1, say the checks are needed for the wgpu GLES HAL, not because the spec requires them.
>
> ---

#### [#10155](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10155): perf(core): reuse the acceleration structure build scratch buffer ([@mstampfli](https://github.com/mstampfli))
- **Verdict:** Needs a bit of work
- **Waiting on:** author. On 2026-09-04 Vecvec asked for a policy that releases or shrinks the cached buffer after many builds that use only a small part of it. On 2026-10-06 Vecvec asked "are you still keen to pursue this". The author has not answered.
- **Last activity:** 2026-10-06, Vecvec, asked the author whether they still want to continue. 31 days. The author's last activity was 2026-09-03.
- **Approvals:** none
- **CI:** green (head of 2026-09-02; stale against trunk)
- **Summary:** Keeps the largest scratch buffer used for acceleration-structure builds on the device, and reuses it in later builds. This avoids one large device allocation and free per build.
- **Findings:**
  1. [medium] `wgpu-core/src/scratch.rs` (Drop) The cache keeps the largest buffer ever used until device loss or drop, with no trim. Both reviewers (inner-daemons, Vecvec) object to this, and the open design question is still the shrink/eviction policy.
  2. [low] `wgpu-core/src/device/resource.rs` `release_gpu_resources` destroys the cached buffer. A `ScratchBuffer` that retires after that call puts a buffer back into the cache, which then stays until `Device::drop`. This is harmless, but the comment "a lost device will not build again" is not the full story.
  3. [low] There is no automated test for reuse. The author verified reuse only with local instrumentation.
- **To land:** the author answers Vecvec and adds a retention/shrink policy (or the maintainers accept unbounded peak retention); rebase.
- **Move-along action:** close (stale), unless the author answers soon. The author has not replied in 31 days, and the design is not settled. Alternatively, ping the author one last time with a deadline.
- **Depth:** full

#### [#10675](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10675): fix(core): hold `command_indices` around HAL surface (un)configure and queue drop ([@AdrianEddy](https://github.com/AdrianEddy))
- **Verdict:** Land as is

- **Approvals:** sagudev
- **CI:** green
- **Summary:** Holds `Device::command_indices.write()` during HAL surface configure/unconfigure and `Queue::drop`'s `wait_for_idle`. This serializes those calls with submit/present, and stops the multi-threaded `vkDeviceWaitIdle` race and the related crashes.
- **Findings:**
  1. [low] Lock ordering looks correct:
     - `Device::maintain` takes `command_indices.read()` in a scope and releases it before a `Queue` can drop, so `Queue::drop` cannot deadlock on the same thread.
     - `Queue::drop` releases the guard before it takes the snatch lock and before `handle_hal_error`.
     - The new rank edges `SURFACE_PRESENTATION` → `DEVICE_COMMAND_INDICES` and `INSTANCE_DEVICES` → `DEVICE_COMMAND_INDICES` match the code paths.

     No issue found.
  2. [low] Submits and presents on other threads now wait while a swapchain is created during resize. This is the same as Dawn, and it is a deliberate trade-off.
  3. [low] There is no regression test, only an out-of-tree repro. A deterministic test is hard to write here, so this is acceptable.
  4. [low] Policy: the checklist says that an agent both created the PR and wrote the description. inner-daemons objected to the LLM-written description. Per CONTRIBUTING, fully agentic PRs are not allowed. The author says that they understand the change, and they defended it in their own words. This is a maintainer judgment call, not a code problem.
- **To land:** nothing (a member approval).
- **Move-along action:** review and merge, or ping jimblandy (the assignee). First decide whether the agent-disclosure checklist is acceptable under the agentic-PR policy.
- **Depth:** full

#### [#10776](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10776): Multi-planar texture copy validation ([@andyleiserson](https://github.com/andyleiserson))
- **Verdict:** Needs a bit of work (it is blocked on [#10710](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10710); the code itself looks ready for review)
- **Waiting on:** maintainer. No reviewer is assigned and there are no reviews or comments. The PR is also blocked on [#10710](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10710).
- **Last activity:** 2026-10-28, andyleiserson, opened and pushed. On 2026-11-02 the PR was updated, probably when the `merge: rebase` label was added (the actor is not visible). 9 days since the last code activity.
- **Approvals:** none
- **CI:** green
- **Summary:** Folds `extract_texture_selector` into `validate_texture_copy_range`, validates copy extents in plane texels for single-plane copies (plane 0 texels plus chroma-subsampling alignment for all-plane copies), and allows B2T/T2B and copy-in for one plane of a multi-planar texture.
- **Findings:**
  1. [medium] It depends on [#10710](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10710). It must be rebased after [#10710](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10710) merges. The label `merge: rebase` is already set.
  2. [low] `wgpu-core/src/command/transfer.rs` (T2T aspect check) `CopySrc/DstMissingAspects` now apply only to depth-stencil formats. This is correct, because `copy_format` makes multi-planar `All` copies require the same format on both sides, and a single-plane copy requires the matching plane format. A reviewer should confirm that the HAL backends (Vulkan, DX12) support copy *into* one plane. Metal has no multi-planar support.
  3. [low] Zero-init safety for plane writes: `has_copy_partial_init_tracker_coverage` treats any copy with an aspect other than `All` as partial. So writing one plane first clears the whole subresource and does not mark the other plane initialized. Uninitialized memory is not exposed.
  4. [low] `wgpu-types/src/texture.rs` `plane_size` adds `assert_eq!(plane.is_some(), is_multi_planar)`. This also runs for the existing `compute_render_extent` callers. Those callers are guarded (views of a whole multi-planar texture are rejected earlier), but this is a new panic site in a public `#[doc(hidden)]` function.
- **To land:** merge [#10710](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10710), then rebase and review.
- **Move-along action:** review, or assign a reviewer (ErichDonGubler reviewed [#10710](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10710) and has the context) once [#10710](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10710) lands.
- **Depth:** targeted (core and types transfer/queue diff read; the 920 lines of new tests were skipped)

**Deeper review ([#10776](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10776): Multi-planar texture copy validation):**

> - **Open questions from the first pass:**
>   1. Read the skipped tests (about 920 lines).
>   2. Check the validation for holes: copies between planes of different formats, partial copies, and zero-init tracking per plane.
> - **Answers:**
>   1. **Tests read** (the validation tests in `tests/tests/wgpu-validation/api/texture.rs` +592, and the GPU tests in `tests/tests/wgpu-gpu/planar_texture/mod.rs` +328).
>      - **Validation tests** (noop device):
>        - compatible T2T pairs: NV12/P010 `All`↔`All`, plane↔matching single-plane format in both directions, plane↔same plane;
>        - incompatible pairs;
>        - aspects that do not exist;
>        - per-plane bounds at several mip levels, with exact overrun errors;
>        - all-plane bounds and 2x2 alignment;
>        - texture-buffer copies per plane (`to_buffer`, `from_buffer`, `write_texture`), plus rejection of `All` and of nonexistent planes.
>      - **GPU tests:**
>        - a partial, offset all-plane T2T copy with each plane read back (NV12, P010);
>        - copies into one plane from a single-plane texture and from another planar texture, checking that only the target rectangle changed (NV12, P010).
>      - The coverage is good.
>      - **Gaps:**
>        - no GPU test writes only one plane of a fresh texture and then reads the other plane (zero-init per plane);
>        - `copy_buffer_to_texture` into a plane is only validation-tested, because the GPU tests fill planes with `queue.write_texture`;
>        - no GPU test uses array layers or mip levels above 0;
>        - I did not check whether any CI adapter supports `TEXTURE_FORMAT_NV12` (unknown). If none does, the GPU tests are skipped on CI.
>   2. **Validation holes:**
>      - **Planes of different formats** [no hole]. `copy_format` (`wgpu-core/src/command/transfer.rs:1543-1578`) resolves each side to the plane format (single-plane aspect) or to the texture format (`All`), and requires equality. Plane0 (R8) → Plane1 (Rg8) is rejected. NV12 `All` → R8 is rejected. P010 `All` → NV12 `All` is rejected. A copy in the same texture from plane 0 to plane 1 is rejected for the format mismatch. Plane0→Plane0 in the same texture goes through `validate_copy_within_same_texture`.
>      - **Partial copies** [no hole]. Single-plane copies are bounds-checked against `plane_size` in plane texels (`transfer.rs:509-520`). All-plane copies are checked in plane-0 texels with `size_multiple_requirement` (2x2) alignment (`transfer.rs:496-506`), so the subsampled regions are exact. In the HAL region split (`transfer.rs:1681-1709`), origin and size are divided by the subsampling factors, which is exact because of that alignment.
>      - The `debug_assert!(width % w_subsampling == 0)` in `plane_size` holds at every mip level, because `create_texture` requires multi-planar sizes to be a multiple of `2 << (mips-1)` (`device/resource.rs:1936-1961`).
>      - An `All` copy to or from a buffer is rejected by `CopyAspectNotOne`.
>      - **Zero-init per plane** [no hole]. The first pass said writing one plane "does not mark the other plane initialized". That is **refuted**. The init tracker is per (mip, layer), not per aspect. `has_copy_partial_init_tracker_coverage` returns true for any aspect other than `All` (`wgpu-core/src/init_tracker/texture.rs:17-28`). So a plane write first clears the whole subresource (all planes, with the [#10710](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10710) clear fix included in this branch) and then marks it initialized. The other plane reads zeros, not uninitialized memory. This costs one extra clear per first plane write, which is acceptable.
> - **New findings:**
>   1. [low] The first commit of the branch (`62998461`) is [#10710](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10710), which is still open. The PR includes it, so the PR cannot merge before [#10710](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10710) merges and this PR is rebased. This is unchanged from the first pass.
> - **Verdict change:** none. The code looks ready for review once [#10710](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10710) lands.
> - **Move-along action change:** none. Optionally, ask for a GPU test that writes only plane 1 of a fresh texture and reads plane 0 as zeros.
>
> ---

#### [#10812](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10812): [core] Add child texture concept that shares underlying data and subsets TextureUsage ([@sagudev](https://github.com/sagudev))
- **Verdict:** Needs a lot of work
- **Waiting on:** maintainer. No reviewer is assigned and there are no reviews or comments.
- **Last activity:** 2026-11-02, sagudev, opened. 4 days.
- **Approvals:** none
- **CI:** green
- **Summary:** Adds `Texture::create_child_texture` (wgpu-core only), which makes a texture handle with `TextureInner::Child { parent }`. The child has the same descriptor as the parent but a subset of its usage, and it shares the parent's init tracker, tracking index, clear mode, views and bind groups through `Arc`s.
- **Findings:**
  1. [high] `wgpu-core/src/resource.rs:1818` (`impl Drop for Texture`) `clear_mode` is now a shared `Arc<RwLock<..>>`, but every `Texture::drop`, including the child's, destroys the `RenderPass`/`Surface` clear views with `ManuallyDrop::take`. When a child of a texture that has `RENDER_ATTACHMENT` (or a depth format) is dropped, the parent's clear views are destroyed. A later lazy clear of the parent then uses freed HAL views, and dropping the parent calls `ManuallyDrop::take` again on the same slots. That is a double free and undefined behavior, reachable from a safe public API. The tests use only COPY/TEXTURE_BINDING usage, so they miss this. Only the owner (the Native texture) may destroy the clear views.
  2. [medium] The design shares the internals by wrapping five fields in `Arc` for every texture. This adds an allocation and an indirection to all textures, to support a feature that only Firefox and Servo use. Every other field and drop path now has to be audited for "parent vs. child" semantics (finding 1 is the first problem found). A design discussion (for example, `Child` holding only the parent `Arc` and forwarding to it) is needed before a line-by-line review.
  3. [medium] The shared `tracking_data` means that the parent and its children have the same tracker index, while the trackers keep one `Arc<Texture>` per index. That works for lifetime, because a child holds its parent, but usage checks and error labels may report whichever handle was inserted first. This needs tests where the parent and a child are used in the same pass or usage scope (for example, a usage conflict between a child view and a parent view).
  4. [low] `create_child_texture` writes no trace action, but its drop records `DropTexture`, so trace replay breaks. The `View Formats` subset error prints the parent's `format_features` and not the parent's `view_formats`.
  5. [low] There is no CHANGELOG entry. This is acceptable for a change only in wgpu-core.
- **To land:** fix the ownership of the clear views (only the root texture destroys them), add tests with `RENDER_ATTACHMENT` and depth parents and with mixed parent/child usage, settle the design question about the `Arc` fields, and add a trace action.
- **Move-along action:** review, starting with finding 1 and the design question, and decide whether the shared-`Arc` design is acceptable for [#5138](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/5138) before a detailed review.
- **Depth:** targeted (the core diff was read in full; the 284 lines of tests were only checked for coverage)

**Deeper review ([#10812](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10812): [core] Add child texture concept that shares underlying data and subsets TextureUsage):**

> - **Open questions from the first pass:**
>   1. Does dropping a child destroy the parent's shared clear views, which then causes a use-after-free and a double free?
>   2. What do the tests not cover?
>   3. Is the design acceptable (five texture fields wrapped in `Arc` for every texture)?
> - **Answers:**
>   1. **[confirmed]** Exact drop path, at the PR head:
>      - `create_child_texture` (`wgpu-core/src/resource.rs:2051`) copies `clear_mode: Arc::clone(&self.clear_mode)` (`resource.rs:2138`). The parent and every child share one `TextureClearMode`.
>      - `create_texture` gives the parent `TextureClearMode::RenderPass { clear_views }` for any texture with depth/stencil write usage, or with `RENDER_ATTACHMENT` and dimension `D2` (`wgpu-core/src/device/resource.rs:2132-2194`). Surface textures get `TextureClearMode::Surface { clear_view }`.
>      - `impl Drop for Texture` (`resource.rs:1802`) runs for every texture, the child included. Without a parent/child check it calls `ManuallyDrop::take` on each clear view and `destroy_texture_view` (`resource.rs:1818-1841`). The enum stays `RenderPass`/`Surface`, and the slots now hold moved-out `Box`es.
>      - The child holds `TextureInner::Child { parent: Arc<Texture> }`, so the child always drops before the parent's last reference. Two outcomes:
>        - (a) The parent is still alive. A later lazy clear of the parent (`command/clear.rs:549-577`, `Texture::get_clear_view`) uses a freed HAL view. This is a use-after-free.
>        - (b) The parent drops. The parent's `Drop` matches the same variant and calls `ManuallyDrop::take` plus `destroy_texture_view` on the same pointers again. This is a double destroy and a double `Box` free. It happens even with no GPU work: create a `RENDER_ATTACHMENT` (or depth) 2D parent, create one child, then drop both.
>      - **Reachable from safe code.** `wgpu::Texture::as_core()` and `wgpu::Texture::from_core()` are safe public functions (`wgpu/src/api/texture.rs:207,214`), and `create_child_texture` is `pub`. The new tests call exactly this path.
>      - A child of a surface texture (reachable through `as_core()` on the surface texture) hits the same double free on `TextureClearMode::Surface`.
>   2. **Tests** (read in full: `tests/tests/wgpu-gpu/child_texture.rs`, 112 lines; `tests/tests/wgpu-validation/api/texture.rs`, +172 lines). They cover: a usage-subset error, a size-mismatch error, a successful create, `usage()` reporting, view creation failing after `child.destroy()`, the parent and a sibling still working after one child is destroyed, `write_texture` failing on a child without `COPY_DST`, and (GPU) content written through child A that child B reads back after A is destroyed. Every parent in the tests has only `COPY_SRC | COPY_DST` usage, which gives `BufferCopy` clear mode. That is why finding 1 is not caught. The tests do not cover:
>      - `RENDER_ATTACHMENT`, depth/stencil or surface parents (this would expose answer 1);
>      - drop order (drop the child, then use or drop the parent);
>      - `parent.destroy()` followed by use of a child (the doc comment says this destroys all children; untested);
>      - a child view in a bind group or render pass; a usage conflict between a parent view and a child view in one usage scope;
>      - destroying a child after encoding and before submit (see new finding 1);
>      - mismatch errors for mip count, sample count, dimension, format and `view_formats` (only `Usage` and `Size` are tested);
>      - a child of an invalid (error) parent; a child of a child;
>      - zero-init: a read through a child of a texture that was never written;
>      - trace record and replay.
>   3. **Design view.** Sharing state is the right idea, but five separate `Arc`s are the wrong shape, and the shape caused answer 1.
>      - Each texture now does five extra heap allocations, and each access to the init tracker, clear mode, views, bind groups and tracking index goes through one more pointer. All textures pay this, for a feature that only Firefox and Servo use.
>      - Ownership is split. The `Arc`s share the state, but `Drop` and `destroy` still act as if each handle owns that state. Every field and every drop or destroy path must be re-audited for "parent vs. child". Answer 1 and new finding 1 are the first two misses.
>      - Better shape: put the owned state (raw `TextureInner`, clear mode, init tracker, tracking data, views, bind groups) into one `Arc<TextureShared>` (one allocation) whose `Drop` destroys the HAL objects exactly once. `Texture` then becomes a handle with `desc`, its own usage and its own snatchable "expired" flag. A child is a second handle on the same `TextureShared`. This removes the double free by construction and costs one allocation, not five.
>      - The other option is the issue's original idea ([#5138](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/5138)): "rename" by snatching the HAL texture into a new `Texture`. The author says this caused problems with pending submissions and deferred destruction.
>      - Either way, the maintainers should agree on the shape before a line-by-line review.
> - **New findings:**
>   1. [medium] **A destroyed child is not caught at submit when another handle with the same tracker index came first.**
>      - The parent and children share `TrackingData`, so they share one tracker index.
>      - In the texture tracker, `insert_or_merge` and `insert_or_barrier_update` (`wgpu-core/src/track/texture.rs:1023,1079`) store the `Arc<Texture>` only when the index is not owned yet. The first handle inserted wins, and later handles only merge state.
>      - `validate_command_buffer` (`wgpu-core/src/device/queue.rs:2265-2285`) checks only the stored handle with `try_inner_nested`.
>      - Sequence: encode a copy into child B (or the parent), encode a copy into child A, call `A.destroy()`, then submit. The submit passes, and A's copy runs on the recycled texture.
>      - [#5138](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/5138) wants to prevent exactly this ("old references to the expired textures cannot affect the recycled versions").
>      - Bind groups are safe here, because `BindGroup::try_raw` checks each bound view's own texture (`binding_model.rs:1757-1766` at the PR head).
>   2. [low] Child `destroy()` snatches `TextureInner::Child { parent }` while it holds the snatchable write lock (`resource.rs:1991-1999`). If the user already dropped the parent handle, the parent's `Drop` runs under that lock. The lock ranks allow this (`lock/rank.rs:139-160`), so this is a note, not a bug.
>   3. [low] `create_child_texture` does not call `device.check_is_valid()` and does not check that the parent is not destroyed. A child of a destroyed parent is created as `Valid` and fails only when it is used.
> - **Verdict change:** none (Needs a lot of work). The high finding is confirmed, reachable from safe `wgpu` API, and a second ownership hole (new finding 1) exists.
> - **Move-along action change:** none. Add to the review: the single-`Arc<TextureShared>` shape as the suggested fix, and new finding 1.
>
> ---

## Core, API and tooling


Date of review: 2026-11-06. CI states come from the check runs on each PR's head commit.

#### [#10821](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10821): Release the acquired surface texture when the device is lost ([@KanikaBreed](https://github.com/KanikaBreed))
- **Verdict:** Needs a bit of work
- **Waiting on:** author (to answer sagudev's question about [#10296](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10296))
- **Last activity:** 2026-11-03, sagudev, asked how this relates to [#10296](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10296) (merged) and said the surface should be unconditionally unconfigured. No author reply. 3 days.
- **Approvals:** none
- **CI:** green
- **Summary:** In `Surface::present` and `Surface::discard`, if the device is lost, the PR drops `acquired_texture` before it returns the error. Without this, a lost device keeps the texture.
- **Findings:**
  1. [medium] `wgpu-core/src/present.rs:315` The motivation may already be covered on trunk. Since [#10296](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10296), `configure` and `Surface::drop` call `unconfigure_inner` (`wgpu-core/src/instance.rs:868`). That function takes and destroys `acquired_texture` and ignores device-lost errors. The description says the surface "can't be reconfigured or dropped", and that is not obviously true now. The author must say which path still leaks. The 211 to 67 MiB measurement may come from the `Arc<Texture>` being dropped earlier, not from a fix to reconfigure or drop. This is sagudev's open question.
  2. [low] `wgpu-core/src/present.rs:426` The texture is dropped (`= None`), but `unconfigure_inner` calls `texture.destroy()`. For consistency, consider `take()` plus `destroy()`, or reuse `release_inner`. The discard path also has no comment, but the present path has one.
  3. [low] There is no test. The author says the GPU suite has no surface. A lost-device test with a surface is hard, so this is acceptable if the maintainer agrees.
  4. [low] There is no CHANGELOG entry, but this is a user-visible fix for device-loss recovery. Process: the "automatically created by an Agent" box is checked. A human directed the work and wrote parts of the description, so it is not fully agentic.
- **To land:** answer the [#10296](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10296) question. Show that the fix is still needed on trunk, or close the PR. Optionally use `destroy()` for consistency and add a CHANGELOG line.
- **Move-along action:** ping author (the [#10296](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10296) question)
- **Depth:** full

**Deeper review ([#10821](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10821): Release the acquired surface texture when the device is lost):**

> - **Open questions from the first pass:**
>   1. How does this PR relate to the merged [#10296](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10296) (sagudev's question)?
>   2. Does trunk already release the acquired surface texture on device loss in all paths (configure, drop, present, get_current_texture)?
>   3. Is this PR still needed?
> - **Answers:**
>   1. **[answered]** [#10296](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10296) ("Implement unconfigure in wgc and use it for configure", merged 2026-09-11, merge commit `91fe2f95`) is already in this PR's base (`837694b8`; the compare API reports the base 247 commits ahead with 0 behind). The author therefore made and measured this change on a tree that already has [#10296](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10296).
>   2. Trunk, per path:
>      - **configure** [released]. `Surface::configure` checks the *new* device (`instance.rs:999`) and then calls `unconfigure_inner` (`instance.rs:1007`). That function `take`s `acquired_texture` (`instance.rs:874`) and ignores device-lost errors from `maintain`. Reconfiguring with a new, valid device releases the old texture. Reconfiguring with the same lost device fails before that point, as expected.
>      - **unconfigure and Surface drop** [released]. `Surface::unconfigure` (`instance.rs:915-924`) and `impl Drop for Surface` (`instance.rs:1038-1050`) call `unconfigure_inner` unconditionally.
>      - **present** [not released]. `present_inner` returns on `check_is_valid()?` (`present.rs:315`) and keeps `acquired_texture`.
>      - **discard** [not released]. `discard_inner` returns on `check_is_valid()?` (`present.rs:426`) and keeps it. This is also the path of `wgpu::SurfaceTexture::drop` (`texture_discard`).
>      - **get_current_texture** [nothing to release]. It returns early on a lost device (`present.rs:182`). The PR does not change it either.
>      - **release** [released]. `release_inner` takes the texture without a device check (`present.rs:464-479`).
>   3. **[mostly not needed]** The description says the surface "can't be reconfigured or dropped". That is refuted for trunk and for the PR's base: configure with a new device, unconfigure and drop all release the texture. The only effect of the PR is timing. The held `Arc<Texture>`, and with it the `Arc<Device>` of the lost device, is released at the failing present/discard and not at the next configure/unconfigure/drop. The 211→67 MiB result can come from this earlier release, but only if the app keeps the old surface unconfigured for a while. That is not shown.
> - **New findings:**
>   1. [low] sagudev's suggestion (unconfigure the surface unconditionally when the device is lost) covers this PR's case and also the HAL swapchain. If the maintainers want earlier release, `present`/`discard` on a lost device should call `unconfigure_inner`, not just set `acquired_texture = None`. That avoids a third release style next to `destroy()` and `release_inner`.
> - **Verdict change:** Needs a bit of work → Needs a bit of work, leaning to close. On trunk, the stated bug does not reproduce from the code.
> - **Move-along action change:** ping the author. Ask them to reproduce on trunk with `surface.configure(new_device)` and say which path still holds the old device. If they cannot, close, or rework to sagudev's unconditional-unconfigure approach.
>
> ---

#### [#10831](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10831): Fix truncated trace playback ([#1620](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/1620)) ([@vatsanbalaji-ossagent](https://github.com/vatsanbalaji-ossagent))
- **Verdict:** Needs a bit of work. Policy question first.
- **Waiting on:** maintainer. ErichDonGubler committed to a full review "early next week" (2026-11-05). They also asked for a design decision: is losing pretty-printed traces OK?
- **Last activity:** 2026-11-05, author account, pushed "Address review feedback" and replied to Erich. 1 day.
- **Approvals:** none (Erich left 8 COMMENTED reviews)
- **CI:** green on head f36a11e. The earlier Clippy failure is fixed.
- **Summary:** `DiskTrace` now writes one compact RON action per line and ends the file with `// End of trace` instead of `]`. `player::parse_trace` parses each line on its own and skips an unparsable last line. The old list format is no longer readable.
- **Findings:**
  1. [high, policy] This looks like a fully agentic PR. The account `vatsanbalaji-ossagent` is a dedicated agent account. It opened the PR, wrote all three commits, and wrote the review replies in agent style. A human ([@vatsanbalaji](https://github.com/vatsanbalaji)) claims to have "reviewed and approved" it, but has not commented on the PR. AGENTS.md does not allow fully agentic PRs. CONTRIBUTING.md (LLMs section) asks that descriptions and replies be written by the contributor. The maintainers must decide whether the human must take ownership from their own account, or whether to close the PR.
  2. [medium, design] `wgpu-core/src/device/trace/record.rs:98` The change from pretty RON to compact RON makes traces hard to read and to edit by hand. Erich: "a deliberate design choice, and we need to decide whether this is okay before merging". This is still open.
  3. [medium] The trace format break is user-visible, and old traces cannot be played. This may matter for Firefox trace users. It is documented in CHANGELOG, but no maintainer has accepted the break yet. A small fallback (detect a leading `[`) would be cheap. The author removed the fallback at Erich's request, so this is a decision, not a defect.
  4. [low] `player/src/lib.rs:46` Erich asked to remove the special last-line match arm. The author pushed back, because that arm is the actual fix. The author is right. The thread is still unresolved.
  5. [low] `player/src/lib.rs` Comments work only as whole `//` lines or trailing `//` comments. A multi-line `/* */` comment breaks parsing. Doc it or accept it.
- **To land:** resolve the authorship policy question. Decide between pretty and compact RON. Get Erich's full review.
- **Move-along action:** decide whether this agent-account PR is acceptable under the no-fully-agentic policy (if not, ask [@vatsanbalaji](https://github.com/vatsanbalaji) to own it from their account, or close). Then let Erich finish their review.
- **Depth:** full

#### [#10838](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10838): Fix sparse tlas instance indices ([@Vecvec](https://github.com/Vecvec))
- **Verdict:** Needs a bit of work
- **Waiting on:** author (CI red because of a bug in the PR)
- **Last activity:** 2026-11-06, Vecvec, pushed new commits. No human comments or reviews yet. 0 days.
- **Approvals:** none
- **CI:** red, and the failure is related. `Test Linux x86_64` fails in `wgpu_gpu::ray_tracing::pipelines::{empty_pipeline_use, intersection_index_bounds}` on Vulkan/llvmpipe. The cause is a panic at `wgpu-core/src/command/ray_tracing.rs:251` (`expect_failed`).
- **Summary:** In a TLAS, each `None` slot before the last `Some` now becomes a null instance, so shader instance indices match the array index. The null instance uses BLAS handle 0 where that is allowed (Vulkan, DX12 except WARP). On other backends it uses an empty BLAS that is built at device creation. The PR adds `Alignments::null_acceleration_structure_allowed` to hal.
- **Findings:**
  1. [high] `wgpu-core/src/device/resource.rs:~851` and `command/ray_tracing.rs:248` The null AS is created only when `EXPERIMENTAL_RAY_QUERY` is enabled. BLAS and TLAS creation are also allowed with only `EXPERIMENTAL_RAY_TRACING_PIPELINES` (`device/ray_tracing.rs:29-30`). On such a device, every TLAS build panics on `null_blas_handle().expect(...)`. This panics in wgpu-core, which is reachable from safe API, and it is the current CI failure. Initialize the null AS for either feature, or treat a missing cell as handle 0 when `null_acceleration_structure_allowed` is true.
  2. [medium] `wgpu-core/src/null_acceleration_structure.rs:~66` `NonZeroU64::new(sizes.build_scratch_size).expect(".max(1) to ensure it is non-zero")`. The code does not call `.max(1)`. If a backend returns 0 scratch size for an empty BLAS (plausible on Metal), device creation panics. Use `.max(1)`.
  3. [medium] The Metal path (empty BLAS) has no CI coverage. The author has not yet tested it ("I intend to test this on mac today"). The empty-BLAS path is tested only through WARP. Ask for the Metal result.
  4. [low] `command/ray_tracing.rs` The `TlasInstanceCountExceeded` check now uses `instances.len()` (all slots), not the count of `Some` slots. The check is also stricter for wgpu-core callers that pass more slots than `max_instances` with trailing `None`s. This is probably correct, but it is a behavior change that the description does not mention.
  5. [low] Null instances use `mask: 0xff`. Use `mask: 0` as defense in depth, so a ray can never hit the empty or null instance on any driver. The description also says (in a "*" note) that WARP crashes with a null instance and a shared scratch buffer. That is a driver workaround keyed on `is_warp`. It is acceptable, but needs a tracking issue.
- **To land:** fix the feature gating so RT-pipeline-only devices do not panic (this makes CI green). Add `.max(1)` on the scratch size. Report the Metal test result.
- **Move-along action:** ping author (CI failure is the `expect` panic on RT-pipeline-only devices). Assign a ray-tracing reviewer after that.
- **Depth:** targeted. I reviewed the core logic, the hal flag, and the CI log. I did not review the test shader math or the DX12 and Vulkan spec claims in detail.

#### [#10839](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10839): fix: relax render bundle resource lifetimes ([@mvanhorn](https://github.com/mvanhorn))
- **Verdict:** Needs a bit of work

This matches what `RenderPass` already allows. Fixes [#6433](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/6433).
- **Findings:**
  1. [medium] `wgpu/src/util/encoder.rs:8` After the change, the `'a` parameter on the `RenderEncoder<'a>` trait is unused. Either keep it on purpose to limit the break, or remove it. This is a maintainer decision. The change already breaks external implementors (the CHANGELOG says so).
  2. [low] The change is sound. The core backend clones `Arc`s at each call (`backend/wgpu_core.rs:2893+`). The WebGPU backend's buffer `Drop` is a no-op. `RenderPass` already has the same signatures. `set_bind_group` on the bundle already used an independent lifetime.
  3. [low] `tests/tests/wgpu-validation/api/render_bundle.rs` The `bundle_resource_lifetimes` test is mostly a compile-time check, written as a loop over the strings `"inherent"`, `"bundle trait object"` and `"pass trait object"` with `eprintln!`. It is about 150 lines and could be much shorter. `draw_errors_reported_at_finish` is not related to lifetimes, so it is scope creep.
  4. [low] The CHANGELOG entry has no PR link, and it is under `#### General` in the wrong place. The description repeats the same paragraph twice. The checklist is malformed. The Testing section says the local test run aborted. The description says only "AI was used for assistance", and the text reads as LLM-written. CONTRIBUTING asks authors to write descriptions themselves.
- **To land:** re-run the flaky Windows job. Decide what to do with the trait `'a`. Trim the tests and drop the unrelated one. Add the PR link to the CHANGELOG entry.
- **Move-along action:** review (small API change. Decide the trait-lifetime question and ask for test cleanup).

#### [#10802](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10802): [deno] Report external memory size for GPUBuffer and GPUTexture to encourage GC of them ([@sagudev](https://github.com/sagudev))
- **Verdict:** Needs a bit of work
- **Waiting on:** maintainer (no reviewer assigned, no review)
- **Last activity:** 2026-11-01, sagudev, force-pushed and renamed the PR. 5 days.
- **Approvals:** none
- **CI:** green
- **Summary:** In `deno_webgpu`, `createBuffer` and `createTexture` now report their size to V8 as external memory. A weak finalizer subtracts that size again. This applies pressure on the GC so that unused GPU objects are collected. Fixes [#10523](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10523). The PR also makes `wgpu_core::resource::Buffer::check_is_valid` and `Texture::check_valid` public.
- **Findings:**
  1. [low] `deno_webgpu/device.rs` The accounting is fixed at creation time. `destroy()`, unmap (which frees the `data` Vec), and later `mapAsync` allocations do not change the reported size. So the PR over-reports until GC. That is probably acceptable for a GC hint, but a short comment should say so.
  2. [low] `wgpu-core/src/resource.rs:680,1888` This makes two `pub(crate)` validity checks public on wgpu-core types. The names are not consistent (`check_is_valid` and `check_valid`). The change is small, but it widens the wgpu-core API only for deno. Rename both to the same name, or accept as is.
  3. [low] The finalizer setup code is the same for buffer and texture. It could be one helper.
  4. [low] There is no test. That is normal for deno_webgpu GC behavior. No CHANGELOG entry is needed (the change is deno-only).
- **To land:** a reviewer must look at it. Possibly unify the check method names.
- **Move-along action:** review (or assign a deno-familiar reviewer). The PR is small and CI is green.
- **Depth:** full

#### [#10638](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10638): [hal] Link API hal docs from dyn methods ([@sagudev](https://github.com/sagudev))
- **Verdict:** Land as is
- **Waiting on:** other reviewer (jimblandy, assigned 2026-10-21, no review since)
