#### [#10721](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10721): chore(deps): update rust crate lock_api to 0.5.0 ([@renovate&#91;bot&#93;](https://github.com/apps/renovate))
- **Verdict:** Needs a lot of work (as a stand-alone PR). Merge it as part of a joint change with [#10722](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10722).
- **Waiting on:** maintainer (Wumpf is assignee; code port needed first)
- **Last activity:** 2026-10-29, PaulDance (non-member) commented "should be grouped into [#10722](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10722)". 8 days. Renovate force-pushed 2026-11-05 (bot, ignored).
- **Approvals:** none
- **CI:** red. About 50 jobs fail (Clippy on all targets, MSRV, Test, Doctest, cargo-deny, Miri, publish). Format, Zizmor, shader validation and cargo-deny advisories pass. Failures start in seconds on many jobs, so some are likely infra or stale-base (see cross-item note). But the compile break below is real, so expect red after rebase too.
- **Summary:** Bumps workspace `lock_api` 0.4.6 to 0.5.0 (MSRV 1.95, edition 2024, trait changes). Cargo.lock now holds `lock_api` 0.4.14 (from `parking_lot` 0.12.5 and `spin`) and 0.5.0 (from `wgpu-sync`).
- **Findings:**
  1. [high] `wgpu-sync/src/rwlock.rs:76-77` implements `lock_api::RawRwLock` with `type GuardMarker = lock_api::GuardNoSend`. Upstream 0.5.0 replaces `GuardMarker` with per-acquisition `Guard`/`SharedGuard`/`ExclusiveGuard` types and changes unlock to consume them. Also `is_locked`/`is_locked_exclusive` are now required, and `RawRwLockRecursive` traits are removed. The PR changes no source, so `wgpu-sync` cannot compile against 0.5.0. Manual port needed.
  2. [high] `wgpu-sync/src/rwlock.rs:3` wraps `parking_lot::RawRwLock` (0.12.x, implements `lock_api` 0.4 traits) and calls `lock_api::RawRwLock::*` on it. With `lock_api` 0.5 and `parking_lot` 0.12 these are different trait versions, so it fails until `parking_lot` goes to 0.13 ([#10722](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10722)). No-std path uses `Cell<isize>` and has the same trait port.
  3. [medium] `Cargo.toml:165` comment "Minimum version supported by parking_lot 0.12.3" is now wrong. Also the 0.4.6 floor was chosen for Firefox/downstream vendoring. 0.5.0 forces a second `lock_api` copy in any downstream that still has `parking_lot` 0.12 (Firefox vendoring is a point to check).
  4. [low] The `RawMutex`/`RawRwLock` types are `pub use`d from `wgpu-sync` (`lib.rs:20-21`), so the trait change is a semver-visible change for crates that name them. Likely OK (`wgpu-sync` is internal) but check.
- **To land:** Close and replace with one joint PR that bumps both crates, ports `wgpu-sync` to the new raw-lock API (Guard types, required `is_locked*`), updates the Cargo.toml comment, and rebases on trunk. Check MSRV (rust-version is already 1.95, so fine) and the Firefox vendoring story.
- **Move-along action:** decide: close [#10721](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10721) as superseded by a joint port PR (or ask Wumpf to do the port), or leave both parked and set a Renovate `groupName` for `parking_lot`/`lock_api` (PaulDance's suggestion).
- **Depth:** targeted (diff and local `wgpu-sync` source read; upstream changelog from the PR body, not the crate source)

**CI and status check ([#10721](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10721): chore(deps): update rust crate lock_api to 0.5.0):**

> - **Open questions from the first pass:** what breaks `wgpu-sync/src/rwlock.rs`; are the failures real; is MSRV a non-issue.
> - **Answers:**
>   1. [confirmed] Full CI is red: 66 runs, about 51 failure, 12 success, 2 neutral, 1 skipped. Head e2726d8 (2026-11-05).
>   2. [refuted] "Likely infra or stale base". Clippy Linux x86_64 shows 50 real compile errors in `wgpu-sync` (`could not compile wgpu-sync (lib) due to 50 previous errors`): 13 in `wgpu-sync/src/mutex.rs` and 8 in `wgpu-sync/src/rwlock.rs` (the rest are notes in lock_api 0.5.0 sources).
>   3. [confirmed] What breaks (upstream lock_api 0.5.0 changelog, quoted in the PR body; the same API change is in the compile errors):
>      - `RawMutex::GuardMarker` is replaced by `RawMutex::Guard`; rwlocks get `SharedGuard`, `ExclusiveGuard`, `UpgradableGuard` instead of `GuardMarker`. Error E0437 at `wgpu-sync/src/mutex.rs:71` (`type GuardMarker = lock_api::GuardNoSend`) and the same item at `wgpu-sync/src/rwlock.rs:77`. E0046 "missing: `Guard`" at `mutex.rs:70`, and "missing: `SharedGuard`, `ExclusiveGuard`" for the rwlock.
>      - Unlock operations take the guard state: E0050 `unlock` has 1 parameter, trait has 2 (`mutex.rs:102`); `unlock_exclusive` (`rwlock.rs:113`), `unlock_shared` (`rwlock.rs:165`), `downgrade` (`rwlock.rs:222`) the same.
>      - `RawMutex::is_locked` and `RawRwLock::{is_locked, is_locked_exclusive}` become required (wgpu-sync already implements them at `mutex.rs:114`, `rwlock.rs:187,198`, so no break here).
>      - `lock_api::RawRwLock::INIT` use in `RawRwLock::new` (`rwlock.rs:~100`) fails E0277/E0308 because `parking_lot 0.12`'s `RawRwLock` implements lock_api 0.4, not 0.5. `parking_lot::RawMutex: lock_api::RawMutex` and `RawMutexTimed` are not satisfied at `mutex.rs:36,79,94,106,117,132-141`.
>      - The `RawRwLockRecursive` traits are removed and `const_new` is removed; wgpu-sync does not use them (grep of `wgpu-sync/src/rwlock.rs` and `mutex.rs` at trunk).
>   4. [confirmed] The `lock_api` bump alone cannot work: `parking_lot 0.12.x` still implements lock_api 0.4, so the two crates must move together. cargo-deny also fails: "found 2 duplicate entries for crate 'lock_api'".
>   5. [refuted] "MSRV is a non-issue because `rust-version = 1.95`". `Core MSRV Check Linux x86_64` fails with `rustc 1.87.0 is not supported by the following package: lock_api@0.5.0 requires rustc 1.95`. `wgpu-sync/Cargo.toml:15` sets 1.87 on purpose (for Firefox). CI env has `WGPU_MSRV: 1.87`, `CORE_MSRV: 1.87`. The bump would raise the core MSRV from 1.87 to 1.95 (upstream: "Raised the MSRV to Rust 1.95 and upgraded all crates to edition 2024"). This is a policy decision for the maintainer, not a rebase issue.
> - **New findings:** [high] MSRV policy conflict as in answer 5. [low] The upstream also removed automatic eventual fairness (`UnparkResult::be_fair`) and `hardware-lock-elision`; behavior may change under contention, which is relevant to deadlock-detection tests.
> - **Verdict change:** none ("Needs a lot of work", close or merge as a joint PR), but the reason changes: the blocker is the MSRV policy and the API port, not "infra".
> - **Move-along action change:** the first pass's action "decide: close, or relax range" should add the MSRV question first: is raising core MSRV to 1.95 acceptable for Firefox?

- **Verdict:** Needs a lot of work
- **Waiting on:** maintainer (Wumpf is assignee; code port needed)
- **Last activity:** 2026-10-29, PaulDance commented (see below). 8 days. Renovate push 2026-11-05 (bot).
- **Approvals:** none

- **Summary:** Bumps `parking_lot` 0.12.3 to 0.13.0. Lockfile now has two `parking_lot` (0.12.5 and 0.13.0), two `parking_lot_core` (0.9.12 and 0.10.0), two `lock_api`, and drops `petgraph` 0.6.5/`fixedbitset` 0.4.2. Pulls `redox_syscall` 0.9.5.
- **Findings:**
  1. [high] Same `wgpu-sync` trait break as [#10721](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10721) (`wgpu-sync` takes `lock_api` 0.4.14 and `parking_lot` 0.13.0 in this PR's lock, so the `lock_api` bound in `wgpu-sync/Cargo.toml` and the `parking_lot` RawRwLock disagree). Needs [#10721](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10721) and the code port.
  2. [medium] PaulDance (comment): the MSRV bump to 1.95 is needed. Repo is already at `rust-version = "1.95"`, so this is a non-issue here. They also suggests widening the requirement to `>=0.12.0, <0.14.0` so downstream (Firefox) keeps one copy. Only `const_mutex`/`const_rwlock` are removed in 0.13 and `Mutex::new` is already const in 0.12. Worth deciding. `grep` found no `const_mutex`/`const_rwlock` uses in this repo (checked trunk .rs files), so the breaking-API cost for this repo is only `wgpu-sync`.
  3. [medium] `tests/Cargo.toml:74` enables `parking_lot` `deadlock_detection`. In 0.13 this changes (Once tracking, RwLock tracking fixes), and `parking_lot_core` 0.10.0 pulls `backtrace`/`petgraph`. Lock-rank and deadlock tests could change behavior. Needs a test run.
  4. [low] No CHANGELOG entry needed (internal dep).
- **To land:** Same as [#10721](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10721): single joint PR. Port `wgpu-sync` raw locks. Rebase to get a clean CI signal. Run the deadlock-detection tests.
- **Move-along action:** decide: take PaulDance's relaxed range (`>=0.12, <0.14`) for `parking_lot` so Firefox sees no forced bump, and close both Renovate PRs; or do the joint port. Close/park both with a `renovate` ignore until chosen.
- **Depth:** targeted (diff, local source; no logs)

**CI and status check ([#10722](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10722): chore(deps): update rust crate parking_lot to 0.13.0):**

> - **Open questions from the first pass:** the same; plus is the range `>=0.12, <0.14` workable.
> - **Answers:**
>   1. [confirmed] Full CI red: 66 runs, about 49 failure, 1 cancelled (Test Mac aarch64), 13 success, 2 neutral, 1 skipped. Head b3d53dd (2026-11-05). Clippy WebAssembly Core 1.0 is not in the failure list here.
>   2. [confirmed] Real errors. In Clippy Linux x86_64: `parking_lot::RawMutex: lock_api::RawMutexTimed` / `lock_api::RawMutex` and `parking_lot::RawRwLock: lock_api::RawRwLock` / `RawRwLockDowngrade` are not satisfied. The cause is that `parking_lot 0.13` depends on lock_api 0.5, while the workspace still pins `lock_api = 0.4.6` (`Cargo.toml:166`). So wgpu-sync cannot compile with this PR alone, and its sources must be ported as in [#10721](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10721).
>   3. [confirmed] MSRV: `Core MSRV Check Linux x86_64` fails with `lock_api@0.5.0`, `parking_lot@0.13.0` and `parking_lot_core@0.10.0` each "requires rustc 1.95" (log lines 465-467), against core MSRV 1.87.
>   4. [refuted] A range `>=0.12, <0.14` (or `lock_api >=0.4, <0.6`) is not workable for `wgpu-sync`'s code as it is. `RawMutex`/`RawRwLock` in `wgpu-sync/src/mutex.rs:70-71` and `rwlock.rs:76-77` implement the lock_api trait. In 0.4 the trait has `type GuardMarker` and `unlock(&self)`. In 0.5 it has `type Guard` and `unlock(&self, guard)`. A single `unsafe impl` cannot satisfy both, and the repo has no cfg to select by dependency version. One crate version would have to be chosen by Cargo, which then fails to compile on the other. It would also not give Firefox "one copy" safely: lock_api 0.4 and 0.5 would still both be in the tree whenever parking_lot 0.12 is also used by another dependency (cargo-deny duplicate check). The wider range also does not avoid the 1.95 MSRV once Cargo picks 0.13.
>   5. [unknown] Whether it can be made workable with a `lock_api`-agnostic design (for example no `lock_api` impl, or a small wrapper type per version behind a feature). I did not design this and I would not recommend it as it adds complexity.
> - **New findings:** same as [#10721](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10721) (MSRV 1.87 to 1.95 for `wgpu-sync` / core).
> - **Verdict change:** none, same reason change as [#10721](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10721).
> - **Move-along action change:** PaulDance's range suggestion in the first pass should be dropped. The real options are: (a) joint port of `wgpu-sync` to lock_api 0.5 plus parking_lot 0.13 with the core MSRV raised to 1.95, (b) stay on 0.12 / 0.4 and add a Renovate ignore for both crates until Firefox's toolchain allows 1.95.

Date of review: 2026-11-06. Days are counted from this date. "Last activity" for PRs without comments is the last push or update time from the API; I could not see the commit authors' exact push dates, so treat those as approximate.

## Metal ray tracing set ([#10287](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10287), [#10288](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10288), [#10289](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10289), [#10292](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10292)) and [#9645](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9645)

All four drafts are by [@acoliver](https://github.com/acoliver), opened 2026-09-08/09. They form one stack:

```
trunk
 └─ #10292  Metal AS build sync (seeded fence)   -- supersedes non-draft #9645, fixes #9215
     └─ #10287  Metal ray query semantics + BLAS transform fixes
         └─ #10288  naga: capability for non-uniform AS binding-array indexing
             └─ #10289  Metal: extended AS vertex formats + AS binding arrays
```

- All four PRs target `trunk`, not each other. Each diff therefore also contains the commits below it (file counts grow: 14, 34, 40, 48 files; 2.6k, 4.7k, 5.3k, 6.2k added lines). Real review size per PR is small; review bottom-up.
- [#10292](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10292) replaces non-draft [#9645](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9645) ([@jtbirdsell](https://github.com/jtbirdsell), assignee Vecvec, "Metal AS sync using fences", 68 lines). [#10292](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10292) keeps jtbirdsell's commit as its base and adds the regression tests and a rework to a seeded per-encoder fence. The author says they had no push access to jtbirdsell's branch, so they opened a new PR.
- [#9645](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9645) status: non-draft, mergeable_state "dirty", last activity 2026-08-07 (91 days). Vecvec (member) reviewed it on 2026-06-17 ("code looks good"), confirmed on 2026-06-23 that it fixes the issue on their device but saw a race when splitting the encoder and asked for that to be fixed. No author reply is visible. [#10292](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10292)'s fence rework looks aimed at that race, but I did not verify this.
- Decision for the maintainer that covers the stack: pick [#10292](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10292) or [#9645](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9645) as the vehicle for [#9215](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9215), then close the other. All four stacked drafts show no CI at all (no check runs), and [#10289](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10289)/#10292 are "dirty" (conflicts with trunk).
- On 2026-11-05 [@matthargett](https://github.com/matthargett) commented on [#10289](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10289) asking whether the stack is ready for review/testing and noting that [#10289](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10289) appeared out of draft while the rest are draft. The API shows [#10289](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10289) as draft now. No author reply yet.

#### [#10288](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10288): [naga] require the acceleration structure binding array capability for non-uniform indexing of acceleration structure arrays ([@acoliver](https://github.com/acoliver)) [draft]
- **State:** in progress
- **Waiting on:** author (depends on [#10287](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10287), which depends on [#10292](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10292))
- **Last activity:** 2026-09-09, acoliver, update (58 days).
- **CI:** none.
- **Summary:** Fixes a naga panic (`unreachable!()`) on non-uniform indexing of `binding_array<acceleration_structure>` by returning a validation error, and gates MSL emission on the capability. Adds one snapshot test. It is naga-only and backend-independent, so it could land alone if rebased on trunk.
- **Move-along action:** ping author (ask to rebase this naga fix onto trunk as a standalone PR, since it does not need the Metal changes), then review.

**Deeper review ([#10288](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10288): [naga] require the acceleration structure binding array capability for non-uniform indexing of acceleration structure arrays [draft]):**

> - **Open questions from the first pass:** Is the naga change independent of the stack?
> - **Answers:**
>   1. [confirmed for the analyzer fix; refuted for the PR as written]
>      - The analyzer arm (`naga/src/valid/analyzer.rs:582` at the PR head) can be applied to trunk unchanged. The trunk code at `analyzer.rs:571-585` (HEAD) sends `binding_array<acceleration_structure>` to the buffer branch. There, `global.space` is `Handle`, which hits `_ => unreachable!()`. Any runtime index into such an array panics in validation. This applies to Vulkan and DX12 users with `ACCELERATION_STRUCTURE_BINDING_ARRAY`.
>      - The rest is not independent. The MSL parts (`naga/src/back/msl/mod.rs:905` adds the capability to MSL `supported_capabilities`; `writer.rs:7511` lets AS binding arrays through for MSL 2.4 and later) are Metal enablement work that belongs with [#10289](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10289). The new snapshot `ray-query-binding-arrays` targets `METAL | SPIRV`, and its MSL output depends on the [#10287](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10287) ray-query emission.
> - **New findings:**
>   1. [low] The description says "The MSL writer additionally gates emission ... on the capability". The code gates emission on `lang_version >= 2.4` and adds the capability to MSL. The wording is inaccurate.
>   2. [low] Because the global declaration already requires the same capability (`naga/src/valid/interface.rs:997`), the new check can never fail for a module that validates. It is a panic fix and not new validation. This is correct, but the CHANGELOG line ("Reject ... unless") overstates it.
> - **Verdict change:** none.
> - **Move-along action change:** ask the author for a trunk-based PR that has only the analyzer arm and a SPIR-V-only (or validation-only) snapshot. Move the MSL lines to [#10289](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10289).

**CI and status check:** see the block under [#10287](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10287).

#### [#10289](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10289): [metal] advertise extended vertex formats and acceleration structure binding arrays ([@acoliver](https://github.com/acoliver)) [draft]
- **State:** in progress
- **Waiting on:** author (depends on [#10288](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10288); also a conflict with trunk) and the maintainer for the stack decision
- **Last activity:** 2026-11-05, matthargett (contributor), comment asking if the stack is ready (1 day). Last author activity 2026-09-08 (59 days).
- **CI:** none.
- **Summary:** Enables `EXTENDED_ACCELERATION_STRUCTURE_VERTEX_FORMATS` and `ACCELERATION_STRUCTURE_BINDING_ARRAY` on Metal and raises the per-stage AS limit from 1 to 8. Adds a stride check in wgpu-core on Metal. Large (48 files, +6.2k), 9 commits, "dirty" merge state. Description says Rebase, two commits.
- **Move-along action:** ping author (rebase; reply to matthargett's question) after the stack decision above.

**Deeper review ([#10289](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10289): [metal] advertise extended vertex formats and acceleration structure binding arrays [draft]):**

> - **Open questions from the first pass:** none specific (stack order).
> - **Answers:** n/a.
> - **New findings:**
>   1. [high, reasoned from code] `wgpu-hal/src/metal/adapter.rs:65` raises `MAX_ACCELERATION_STRUCTURES_PER_SHADER_STAGE` from 1 to 8. The budget comment at `:52-61` splits 31 buffer slots: 8 storage + 12 uniform + 8 vertex + 2 internal + 1 AS = 31. `VERTEX_BUFFER_SLOT_START = 23` (`:67`). With 8 AS, a vertex stage that uses the maximum of storage, uniform and AS bindings reaches buffer index 28 before the internal buffers. This overlaps vertex buffer slots 23-30. I found no hal check against `VERTEX_BUFFER_SLOT_START`. The PR text counts only storage and uniform ("31 minus 8 minus 12"). The stale comment "We use the last buffer for an acceleration structure" is still at `:61`.
>   2. [medium] `b5c64227` adds a backend-specific stride rule in wgpu-core (`wgpu-core/src/command/ray_tracing.rs`, `if state.device.backend() == wgt::Backend::Metal`). This is a validation divergence in a crate that processes untrusted input. It is acceptable for a native-only feature, but the maintainer should approve it explicitly.
> - **Verdict change:** none (in progress). Add the slot-budget issue to the author's list.
> - **Move-along action change:** none beyond the first pass. Also ask the author to recompute the per-stage AS limit with vertex buffers included.

**CI and status check:** see the block under [#10287](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10287).

#### [#10292](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10292): [metal] synchronize acceleration structure builds with a seeded fence ([@acoliver](https://github.com/acoliver)) [draft]
- **State:** in progress (the base of the stack)
- **Waiting on:** maintainer (decision: land this instead of [#9645](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9645)) and Vecvec (assignee on [#9645](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9645), tests Metal RT)
- **Last activity:** 2026-09-09, acoliver, opened (58 days).
- **CI:** none. Merge state "dirty".
- **Summary:** Supersedes [#9645](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9645). Fixes [#9215](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9215) (TLAS build reading a BLAS that is still building) with a seeded encoder fence, per-pass AS usage tracking, and reserved command buffer slots; adds regression tests. It is a much bigger change than [#9645](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9645) (14 files, +2.6k). Checklist complete. Tested on M4 Max only.
- **Move-along action:** decide (choose [#10292](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10292) or the smaller [#9645](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9645) for [#9215](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9215); ask Vecvec to review. Needs a rebase either way).

**Deeper review ([#10292](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10292): [metal] synchronize acceleration structure builds with a seeded fence [draft]):**

> - **Open questions from the first pass:**
>   1. Does it replace [#9645](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9645) (is it "superseding" and landable)?
>   2. What does the rework contain?
> - **Answers:**
>   1. [refuted as a landable replacement; confirmed as a superset] It contains [#9645](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9645) (see [#9645](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9645), answer 1) and fixes the cross-command-buffer case. The net diff against `6e8d0bd1` is 14 files, +2588/-102, and it mixes at least five independent changes:
>      - (a) queue-wide seeded `MTLFence` for cross-command-buffer AS ordering (`mod.rs:493-531`, `:582-640`);
>      - (b) fence waits and updates in ray-query compute and render passes. These come from AS bindings or the new `BindGroup::uses_acceleration_structures` (`command.rs:274`, `end_compute_pass`, `end_render_pass`);
>      - (c) `useResource:usage:` for build inputs, scratch and compact-size buffers;
>      - (d) a new `command_buffer_slot.rs` and `MAX_UNSUBMITTED_COMMAND_BUFFERS = MAX_COMMAND_BUFFERS - 1`;
>      - (e) a new `completion.rs`: `Device::wait` uses completed handlers in place of 1 ms polling. This changes every Metal user, not only ray tracing.
>
>      The history is not reviewable commit by commit: `9e7d8b19` adds an MTLEvent chain, and `14f3ea9a` removes it.
>   2. [confirmed] The pieces needed for [#9215](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9215) are (a), optionally (b), and the issue_9215 tests. (c) is defensive. (d) is needed only because (a) allocates internal seed command buffers. (e) is unrelated to [#9215](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9215).
> - **New findings:**
>   1. [medium] `wgpu-hal/src/metal/mod.rs:529` uses `.expect(...)` when the queue fence is missing. The fence is created only when `EXPERIMENTAL_RAY_QUERY` is enabled (`device.rs:747-753`). wgpu-core gates all AS commands and AS bindings on that feature, so this cannot panic from wgpu today. It is a hidden invariant for direct hal users.
>   2. [medium] Every ray-query pass now waits for and updates the single queue fence, so all ray-query passes and AS builds on a queue are serialized on the GPU. The CHANGELOG claims "without serializing unrelated later submissions", which is true only for passes without AS. This needs a performance check before landing.
>   3. [low] `wait_for_acceleration_structure_build` can encode `waitForFence_beforeStages` in the middle of a render pass, at `set_bind_group`, after earlier draws. I did not confirm whether Metal applies a mid-pass render fence wait to the whole pass or only to later draws. Still unknown.
>   4. [low] The new hal unit tests in `wgpu-hal/src/metal/command/tests.rs` (773 lines) need a real Metal GPU with ray tracing. They print `eprintln!` traces from `#[cfg(test)]` hooks in the hot encode paths. These hooks add test-only code in production files.
>   5. [info] No CI has run on this PR (draft, merge state "dirty").
> - **Verdict change:** in progress -> needs to be split. It is not a replacement for [#9645](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9645).
> - **Move-along action change:** after [#9645](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9645) lands, ask acoliver to split the rest:
>   - (1) cross-command-buffer queue fence (a+d) with the separate-command-buffer and pending-writes issue_9215 tests;
>   - (2) ray-query pass waits (b+c);
>   - (3) `completion.rs` poll wakeups as its own Metal PR.
>
>   The tests in `ac6935b1` can go with [#9645](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9645) now.

**CI and status check:** see the block under [#10287](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10287).

- **Summary:** Proposes an extension API for passing backend (hal) data through wgpu-core, to be used first for a DX12 adapter LUID filter wanted by Firefox ([#9932](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9932), [#10011](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10011)). Later commits show other uses (swapchain hooks, present timing, device-creation callbacks, `create_texture_from_raw`). Maintainers agreed on the direction in the maintainers meeting and in comments. Open design questions: `Any` needing `'static`, naming, serde compatibility.
- **Move-along action:** nothing (author is active and has agreed to split it). Maintainer may state final approval on the direction if not already done.

- present-spec done. DXGI questions stay unknown (docs silent); a safe combined fix and author probes are given. [#10293](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10293) spec requirement confirmed (required fix). [#10629](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10629) allocation concern refuted. [#10775](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10775) changes apply_limit_buckets handling (new, user-visible).
- metal-encoders done. [#10506](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10506) ICB ordering bug confirmed (UB even with valid args); new medium on inter-pass timestamps. [#9679](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9679) lifetimes ok; STRICT_WEBGPU_COMPLIANCE device-creation failure (new medium).
- naga-spirv done. [#9295](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9295) both bugs confirmed from code. [#10596](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10596) new medium (As from matrix to bf16 passes validation, hand-built IR only). [#10600](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10600)/#10697 conflict confirmed, about 6 lines plus snapshot regen.
- core done. [#10812](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10812) high confirmed plus a new medium (shared tracker index). [#10821](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10821) leans to close. [#9053](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9053): none of the 12 findings addressed. [#8653](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/8653): commits not split.
- naga-validation done. [#10676](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10676) safe in all backends; new medium override const-fold bug. [#10518](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10518) capability claim refuted: SPIR-V NonWritable members now reach the new analysis. [#10311](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10311) emitter sound; hole confirmed. [#10518](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10518) and [#10311](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/10311) both use capability bit 1 << 46.
- The GitHub MCP server failed to connect for the run 3 agents (400, bad Authorization header). They read GitHub through `gh api` (read only).
- Results were merged as Deeper review / CI and status check blocks under each PR. Summary rows were updated; no verdict bucket changed.
